uniqueness of guard and deadline slacks. The sum-of-squares implication
should be proved over integers, and only then used for natural valuations.
The variable and residual counts become cardinality calculations on the
index types. Python can generate examples, but the mathematical compiler
must itself have a correctness proof.

\paragraph{\code{Liveness/ClockDegrees.lean}.}
Define computable domination and the deadline oracle spectrum on genuine
oracle-recursion semantics. The easy implication from an HIF accepting
schedule to a computable clock uses only the oracle computation of the
$k$th accepting time. The reverse direction should depend on a separately
formalized and audited HIF basis theorem. The c.e.-degree realization can
be formalized independently from that basis theorem: its proof uses the
explicit enumeration modulus and unary-cost bound.

\paragraph{\code{Liveness/Diophantine.lean}.}
Apply \code{Diophantine.mrdp} to the c.e. relations
$\operatorname{Bad}_d$, $\operatorname{BadGap}$, and $V^*$.
Do not try to prove these temporal properties themselves c.e.; that is
exactly what the no-go theorem excludes. Keep the compiler for a concrete
finite trace distinct from a general MRDP witness extractor.

\paragraph{\code{Liveness/AffineRealization.lean}.}
Prove rational push/pop identities and rule-by-rule preservation on the
subset of encoded finite stacks. Only afterward define the arbitrary
completion of the map on the rest of the cube. The proof need not decide
whether every real point is a valid finite stack code. The fourth-coordinate
construction additionally requires the convergent digit series and its
computable inverse on the separated Cantor set.

\subsection{Proof obligations most likely to conceal an error}
The important boundary checks are short enough to list explicitly.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textnormal{(\arabic*)}]
\item Prefix coherence: the length-$d(k)$ certificate must include every
$j\le k$ deadline, not merely the final acceptance count.
\item Finite branching: computable reachability of arbitrary long finite
paths is not itself a substitute for a computably bounded finite successor
list when extracting the effective compact tree.
\item Oracle scope: totality of a chosen schedule program is not equivalent
to existence of an arbitrary schedule; a computable clock does not imply
that its witnessing branch is computable.
\item Slack uniqueness: all inactive top slacks must be forced to zero,
and active quotients must represent a positive sentinel tail.
\item Timing preservation: acceptance flags must have the stated microstep
semantics, especially in the bounded-gap reductions.
\item Domains and arities: natural witnesses, real state coordinates,
finite-horizon variable counts, and fixed MRDP witness dimensions must not
be conflated.
\end{enumerate}
These checks identify the actual mathematical proof boundary. Merely
successfully type-checking an abstract theorem about an incorrectly defined
``trace'' or ``scheduler'' would not establish the intended result.
\section{Connection to ProveIt and a formalization plan\srctag{03}}\label{lbh:qd:sec:proveit}

\subsection{The inspected source boundary}

The repository was inspected at commit
\begin{center}\small\sha\end{center}
from 30 September 2026. The most directly relevant source file is
\begin{quote}\small
\texttt{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Lean/}\\
\texttt{Diophantine/Common/DiophantineTrace.lean}.
\end{quote}
It declares \texttt{boundedForall\_dioph}, \texttt{exactIter\_dioph}, and
\texttt{existsExactIter\_dioph}~\cite{proveit-trace}. The first is a bounded
universal closure theorem, the second handles an exact finite iteration
length, and the third existentially quantifies the finite length.
The repository's Hilbert-tenth README reports MRDP in both directions and
documents the supporting formalization~\cite{qd-proveit-readme}.

These are source-inspection observations. This session did not build the
repository, rerun its axiom audits, or independently validate all claims in
its README. Nothing in the present infinite-path proofs is claimed to be
an existing ProveIt theorem merely because its finite-trace prerequisites
are available there.

\subsection{Suggested module separation}

A sound implementation should keep at least three semantic layers distinct.
The first layer is the finite edge-table language and its complete successor
function. The second is the quadratic polynomial, with a theorem identifying
its natural zero relation with the successor relation. The third is the
space of infinite paths and the different quantifiers used in recurrence,
computable deadlines, and computable paths. Naming the third layer
``Diophantine'' without the qualification ``infinite-path semantics'' would
obscure the main obstruction of this paper.

A proposed dependency order is:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\arabic*.,leftmargin=2em]
\item Define edges, one-hot controls, tagged states, and enabled successors.
Prove that the returned successor list is complete, duplicate free by tags,
and bounded by the source control's syntactic outdegree.
\item Define the residual polynomials and the energy. Prove nonnegativity
on natural assignments; prove that a finite sum of natural products and
integer squares vanishes only when each component vanishes; then prove
\Cref{lbh:qd:thm:compiler} and the coefficient bound.
\item Connect the encoded finite-dimensional relation to the existing
finite-iteration interfaces. Keep state coding, input zero, and natural
versus positive domains explicit.
\item Formalize finite path trees and deadline-respecting subtrees. Establish
\Cref{lbh:qd:thm:deadline} with an explicitly chosen K\"onig-lemma principle,
recording any classical choice used for an infinite branch.
\item Formalize effective simulations, then the arithmetic upper bounds,
recursive-tree reductions, explicit separating trees, and the affine lower
obstruction. A full universal interpreter edge table is a further concrete
implementation task, not a prerequisite for the generic compiler theorem.
\end{enumerate}

The finite compiler theorem should be independently useful even before the
analytical-hierarchy layer is available. Conversely, the compiler must not
be advertised as a formalization of all recurrence results when only local
polynomial identities have been checked.
\section{Relationship to the inspected \repo\ development\srctag{11}}
\label{lbh:ql:sec:repository}

\subsection{Snapshot and mathematical dependencies}
The repository documentation was inspected at immutable commit
\begin{center}
\small\code{e18718e837d43e162252f9a314e8cb797fbd1a1f}.
\end{center}
The relevant entry points are the Hilbert-tenth-problem \code{README.md}
and its Lean \code{MRDP.md} guide \cite{ql-repo-root,ql-repo-mrdp}; the bibliography
provides their complete snapshot paths. The latter states the fixed-finite-polynomial MRDP
interface, its converse, bounded universal quantification, primitive-recursive
graphs, and the availability of separate trace interfaces. It also explicitly
distinguishes the public existence theorem from a practical numerical
polynomial-generation algorithm.

This report uses that distinction rather than obscuring it. The low-degree
local compiler is explicit and executable for finite counter tables. MRDP is
used to situate finite reachability and to identify the obstruction to
existentially representing unrestricted recurrence. The infinite-path and
deadline theorems are independent mathematical developments and have not been
transplanted into the repository's theorem prover.

The inspected documentation records no added axioms for its MRDP endpoint
and describes its audit files. That is the repository's documented status;
this session did not rebuild or independently audit that Lean development.
Only the relevant source documentation was inspected, not every research
article in the repository.

\subsection{A feasible formalization boundary}
A first integration should formalize the finite algebra before attempting
effective descriptive set theory. The natural dependency order is:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item Define the finite edge table, enabling relation, incoming-edge states,
and dummy initialization, using signed polynomial evaluation but natural
counter coordinates.
\item Prove that a natural tuple summing to one is one-hot. Prove the
zero-of-sum lemma for the squares and nonnegative products.
\item Prove the one-step equivalence and path bijection. Establish the degree
and coefficient bounds by the monomial-case audit, not by testing examples.
\item Formalize the finite history and deadline polynomials, including the
unique-slack statement. Connect finite reachability to the existing MRDP API.
\item Separately formalize streams, effective finite branching, and deadline
compactness. Then add the computability-index arguments and the two diagonal
separations.
\end{enumerate}
The first four steps have a small and concrete mathematical interface. The
last step needs a careful representation of partial computations, program
indices, and second-order quantification. Merely introducing a predicate called
``analytic complete'' without the relevant reduction definitions would not
constitute its formalization.

\section{Further research questions and concrete next targets}\label{lbh:sec:questions}\label{lbh:qd:sec:research}\label{lbh:ql:sec:future}

The questions below are proposed research directions. They are not all
asserted to be previously published open problems. Each is a specific
extension or optimization left unresolved by this article, rather than
an invitation to remove a quantifier that the proved hierarchy forbids.

\wtag{} The research questions of the three sources follow, source~10's first. A question of
source~03 or~11 that overlaps an earlier one is printed after it, unchanged, as a paragraph
naming its source and title (``Overlapping question''); the others are printed as questions of their own, with their sources in their titles.
Sources~03 and~11 wrote their questions as subsections; they are printed here as question
environments with unchanged text. Cross-report notes name questions of Reports~A
(\path{canonical-diophantine-certificates}) and~B
(\path{probabilistic-quantum-and-continuous-computation}) of this category by their labels.
\begin{question}[Deadline spectra beyond c.e. cones]\label[question]{lbh:q:spectra}
Which upward-closed classes of Turing degrees occur as
$\operatorname{Spec}_{\mathrm{clk}}(e)$ for a single input of a fixed
two-stack system? In particular, which degrees can be a least element?
\end{question}
\Cref{lbh:thm:ce-spectrum} realizes every principal cone above a c.e. degree.
Its proof uses a computable increasing enumeration and a modulus whose
upper bounds decide the enumerated set. A route beyond the c.e. case is to
study recursive trees whose paths admit domination-based recovery of a
non-c.e. set. One must separately establish that a matching deadline is
computable from that set; an arbitrary limit approximation need not have
a convergence modulus computable from its limit. Nonprincipal spectra
would distinguish ``some sufficiently strong oracle'' from a unique least
clock resource.
\srcnote{\wtag{} Batch~79, 2 October 2026: answered in part by Part~VI (source~12, batch-79
manuscript~04, written as an answer to this question). Every $\Delta^0_2$ degree is a least clock
degree of an input of a fixed two-stack system (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:delta}), by first matching stages,
which avoid the convergence-modulus obstacle described above without contradicting it
(\cref{lbh:cs:thm:firstmatch}); every degree of a unique path through a recursive Baire-space tree is
least (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:singleton}), including $\mathbf0^{(r)}$ and $\mathbf0^{(\omega)}$
(\cref{lbh:cs:thm:jump-tree}); nonprincipal spectra exist (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:nonprincipal}); a least
degree contains a self-modulus and is hyperarithmetic (\cref{lbh:cs:thm:least}), and no nonzero
hyperimmune-free degree is least (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:hif}). Up to uniform computable transformations,
clock problems are the upward effectively closed mass problems (\cref{lbh:cs:thm:closed}). Which
upward-closed classes of degrees occur, and which degrees are least, remain open: source~12 proves
$\mathcal S\subseteq\mathcal L\subseteq\mathcal M\subseteq\{\text{hyperarithmetic}\}$
(\cref{lbh:cs:eq:gap}) without the middle equalities, and its questions
\cref{lbh:cs:q:classification,lbh:cs:q:selfmodulus,lbh:cs:q:minimal} re-scope this one.}

\begin{question}[A verified effective-closed-subclass compiler]
Can a repository implementation accept a certified computable tree
$T$ with $\varnothing\ne[T]\subseteq\cB_e$ and extract the program for a
valid deadline with a proved totality theorem?
\end{question}
\Cref{lbh:prop:closed-clock} supplies the algorithm: search for a tree level
where every node has the required number of accepting visits. The hard
part of a proof assistant implementation is not polynomial arithmetic;
it is packaging the compactness argument that proves termination of every
search. An initial target can avoid HIF degrees entirely and establish a
usable bridge from safety invariants to explicit progress programs.
\srcnote{\wtag{} Batch~79: compare source~12's \cref{lbh:cs:q:verified} (Part~VI), verified
first-match compilation composed with a verified tree interpreter and polynomial compiler.}

\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 03, ``Effective selection inside the deadline fragment'':} \Cref{lbh:qd:thm:cbnotcp} shows that computable deadlines alone do not permit
computable path selection. Which additional conditions on branching,
uniqueness, or extendibility do? For example, a decidable extendibility
predicate would permit recursive choice of a next surviving successor.
The substantive problem is to obtain such a predicate from a natural
syntactic or algebraic condition rather than assume it as an oracle.

\begin{question}[Primitive-recursive and resource-bounded clocks]
What are the sharp index classifications and degree-theoretic analogues
when deadlines are required to be primitive recursive, elementary, or
polynomially bounded in $k$?
\end{question}
A computable deadline permits arbitrarily expensive total computation.
Uniform syntactic families of total clocks change the quantifier accounting:
for an effectively listed total family $(d_m)$, the existence condition has
an immediate $\exists m\forall k$ upper bound, unlike the general
$\sig3$ condition that must verify totality. Establishing sharp lower
bounds requires simulations whose pulse costs fit that family. The HIF
basis argument by itself supplies no elementary or polynomial bound.

\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``Witness spectra beyond computable deadlines'':} \textbf{Question.} Classify recurrent runs whose visit-time functions are
bounded by primitive-recursive, elementary, polynomial, or oracle-computable
functions. A fixed effective family of total clocks gives one kind of index
set; arbitrary program indices with a totality condition give another. The
proof of \cref{lbh:ql:thm:sigma3} suggests a systematic treatment, but the exact
hardness can depend on which clock descriptions are allowed.
\srcnote{\wtag{} Batch~79: compare source~12's \cref{lbh:cs:q:resource} (Part~VI), which asks the
same for its conversion $b\mapsto d_b$ and for native graph, queue and reaction substrates.}

\begin{question}[Clock synthesis under regular reachability abstractions]
Which effective regular abstractions certify a nonempty closed subclass
of accepting schedules, and when do they compute useful deadlines?
\end{question}
To--Libkin-type recurrent reachability procedures and their recent
extensions exploit effective regularity assumptions \cite{ek2025}.
A promising bridge is to turn a regular recurrent witness into a recursive
schedule tree with a quantified progress guarantee. The key task is to
prove that an abstract infinite path corresponds to a concrete path;
a regular overapproximation may contain recurrent behavior absent from
the concrete system. The deadline extraction should be conditional on a
proved simulation or refinement relation, not just apparent recurrence in
an abstraction.

\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``Decidable fragments and certificate extraction'':} \textbf{Target.} Identify structural conditions ensuring that a recurrent run
has a finite lasso or an effectively bounded return schedule, and turn those
conditions into a complete certificate procedure for that class. Existing
regular-model-checking work supplies important positive contrasts to the
unrestricted setting \cite{to-libkin}. A careful bridge should state exactly
which regularity or acceleration hypothesis rules out the diagonal examples.

\begin{question}[Probabilistic and adversarial progress]
How do computable deadlines interact with almost-sure recurrence,
positive-probability recurrence, and strategies that work against every
adversary?
\end{question}
These quantifiers are different from existential selection of one schedule.
Recent regular-model-checking work treats both sure and almost-sure
properties under explicit hypotheses \cite{ek2025}. For the present
framework, one concrete target is a measure-theoretic substitute for the
closed-subclass condition: does a positive-measure computable-deadline
class exist whenever recurrence has a specified effective probability
bound? Another target is to formalize the precise order of strategy,
opponent, time-bound, and probability quantifiers before attempting any
Diophantine compilation.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~B's question \texttt{pqc:q:continuous} (continuous-time and robust
physical semantics). Report~B's Part~I classifies almost-sure termination and probability
thresholds; the deterministic $\ppi2$ row of \cref{lbh:thm:deterministic} is their
non-probabilistic analogue.}
\begin{question}[Minimal geometric dimension and regularity]
Can the clock and c.e.-degree-spectrum realizations be achieved with fewer
coordinates or stronger regularity, while retaining a finite rational
input encoding and an explicitly stated scheduler domain?
\end{question}
The construction here uses three coordinates for a controlled relation
and four for an autonomous map with a scheduler parameter. It does not
claim optimality. Reducing dimension by interleaving codes may destroy
piecewise-affineness or the finite guard partition. Requiring continuity,
invertibility, or a smooth flow creates separate obligations. Any candidate
should first establish a step-exact or computable signal-faithful simulation,
then determine which fixed deadlines or gap bounds survive.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Partly answered for the controlled relation by source~11: its planar realization
(\cref{lbh:ql:prop:planarsim,lbh:ql:cor:planar}) packs the finite control into the integer part
of one coordinate, so the rows it proves (the fixed-system $\an$ row, and the $\sig3$ rows and
separations across systems) hold for a guarded rational piecewise-affine relation on a planar
region instead of $[0,1]^3$. Whether the same packing applied to source~10's fixed two-stack
system carries every row of \cref{lbh:thm:geometry}, the c.e.\ spectra included, on a planar region
(and the autonomous map on three coordinates) is not proved here; the rules of that system act
affinely on each rectangle, which suggests it. Report~B's question ``Reduce the
three-dimensional realizer'' asks for the continuous version, which this does not touch.}
\begin{question}[Robust encodings and noise]
What survives under bounded perturbations, finite precision, or a
noise-tolerant decoding rule?
\end{question}
The current rational codes represent unbounded information at ever finer
scales. Noise can erase low-order digits, so compact exact universality
alone says nothing about robustness. An appropriate problem is to specify
an error model and prove either a robust simulation contract or an
impossibility theorem for a chosen deadline class. This should be treated
as a change of mathematical model, not as an implementation detail of the
exact construction.

\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``Robust planar computation'':} \textbf{Question.} Which of the recurrence distinctions survive under
continuous maps, closed guard conventions, finite perturbation tolerance, or
uniform gaps from decision boundaries? The current exact affine coding does
not establish robustness. An interesting positive theorem would provide
explicit guard margins and a noise model while retaining the intended
observation semantics; an interesting negative theorem would isolate the
compactness principle that makes analytic completeness impossible.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~B's question \texttt{pqc:cb:q:noise} (different noise semantics),
and Report~B's Part~III, whose continuous realizations carry noise radii that the discontinuous
exact maps of \cref{lbh:thm:geometry} and \cref{lbh:ql:prop:planarsim} do not.}
\begin{question}[Other unconventional substrates with timing certificates]
Can one build clock-faithful translations to interaction nets,
contextual rewriting, tiling-based computation, or reaction systems with
an explicit effective zero-test mechanism?
\end{question}
The useful deliverable would be a simulation theorem satisfying
\cref{lbh:thm:transfer}, plus a local polynomial compiler for the target's own
finite steps. Mere finite-halting universality is insufficient. The first
obligations are termination of administrative phases, absence of
accepting internal divergence, and effective bounds on all finite
simulation trees. Restricting to a model with no zero tests may radically
change its computational power, so the operational language must be fixed
before importing universality claims.
\srcnote{\wtag{} Batch~78, 2 October 2026: partly answered, it appears, for reaction systems whose
explicit zero test is an inhibitor, by Part~I of the neighbouring report
\path{quadratic-orthant-certificates} of this category (written in commit \texttt{c51b9880d}; its
source~08, batch-78 manuscript~08, which does not draw the connection itself). Its guarded
register-machine embedding (in its section \texttt{qoc:mp:sec:variants}) simulates a register
machine by maximal-parallel multiset rewriting with one control object: an increment is one rule,
and a decrement-or-branch is a consuming rule together with a rule inhibited by the presence of the
register's object. Checked at this write against the definition before \cref{lbh:thm:transfer}:
the source machine is deterministic, hence effectively finitely branching; each instruction is
exactly one nonempty round, so the blocks have length one, are computable and are uniformly bounded
(bounded gaps transfer as well); with one control object each round is forced, so no target
execution from an encoded state has internal steps or alternative branches; and accepting signals
are read off the control object, one per source visit. One convention is needed: in that Part's
semantics the zero extent is the unique maximal extent at a deadlocked state, so a halted run
continues by empty rounds (its section \texttt{qoc:mp:sec:semantics}). These must count as
termination, as its first-halting certificate (\texttt{qoc:mp:cor:firsthalt}) counts them;
otherwise an accepting halt label would emit infinitely many spurious accepting signals. The local
polynomial compiler for the target's own finite steps is its guarded-round certificate, of degree
two once the guard bits have degree-two certificates (\texttt{qoc:mp:prop:guards}). So the
embedding appears to be a computable signal-faithful block simulation with block length one, and
the question is answered only partly: for register-machine sources and this one class of reaction
systems. A clock-faithful simulation of this report's own fixed universal systems by register
machines is a separate step, not checked here; the embedding is elementary (its source calls it
no improvement on unguarded universality), and guarded histories are not implemented in that
report's shipped code.}

\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``Verified front ends for unconventional substrates'':} \textbf{Target.} Develop marker-preserving front ends from exact graph rewriting,
queue systems, symbolic dynamical maps, and reversible interpreters to the
finite edge table. Each front end should provide a terminating-macro theorem
of the kind used in \cref{lbh:ql:lem:macros}. This prevents a common failure mode:
a correct finite-step simulation that introduces an infinite internal run and
thereby changes a liveness property.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~A's question \texttt{cdc:q:ski} (bridges between combinatory or
graph evaluation and certificates).}
\begin{question}[Smaller exact local certificates]
Can the counts $H(3+R+G)+k$ and $H(4R+E+2G+2)+k$ be improved without
losing the labelled-trace bijection or raising degree above four?
\end{question}
Possible savings include eliminating deterministic rule selectors,
sharing guards when their active conditions are provably disjoint, and
using a different control encoding. Each change should include a new
witness-uniqueness proof; eliminating inactive-slack equations without
an alternative canonicalization is not a valid optimization. Lower bounds
for natural quadratic encodings of guarded affine transitions would also
be informative. The present counts are exact for this compiler, not
optimal across all compilers.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~A's question \texttt{cdc:q:degree} and Report~B's question
\texttt{pqc:q:smaller}. For counter machines, source~11's deadline certificate
(\cref{lbh:ql:cor:deadlinepoly}) already stays at degree two.}
\srcnote{\wtag{} Batch~79: for two-stack traces, source~12's \cref{lbh:cs:thm:quadratic} lowers
the degree to two at the price of more coordinates ($3(T+1)+9mT+k$); its
\cref{lbh:cs:q:guards} asks whether the $8mT$ sign helpers can be reduced.}
\begin{question}[Concrete fixed-arity MRDP extraction]
Can the three c.e. relations used in \cref{lbh:thm:polynomial-signatures} be
compiled to explicit fixed polynomials with audited witness counts,
operation counts, and a verified correspondence to the programs?
\end{question}
The repository's MRDP existence theorem is already enough for the logical
normal forms. A practical extractor would require a compiler for the
particular program semantics and an explicit accounting of all arithmetic
encodings. This project should not begin by promising preservation of
single-fold witnesses: \cref{lbh:rem:singlefold} marks that as a separate and
substantially stronger issue.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~B's questions \texttt{pqc:q:direct} and \texttt{pqc:cb:q:extract}.}
\begin{question}[Quantitative certificates for the degree realizations]
Can one export finite prefixes of the c.e.-degree spectrum construction
as exact quartic certificates together with a formal proof that every
successful deadline computes the intended c.e. set?
\end{question}
This is a focused near-term project combining the most concrete parts of
the article. It needs a standard bounded enumeration machine, the tree
predicate \cref{lbh:eq:c.e.-tree}, a verified unary-cost lower bound, and the
finite compiler. Finite examples alone cannot certify the lower-degree
claim, but they can exercise every arithmetic component used by its proof.

\begin{question}[The mass problem of finding a recurrent schedule]
How do the solution sets $\cB_e$, their nonempty effectively closed
subclasses, and their computable-deadline unions compare under uniform
and nonuniform reductions between mass problems?
\end{question}
\Cref{lbh:thm:core} characterizes nonemptiness of the computably clocked part
through HIF solutions, but it does not provide a uniform algorithm selecting
a schedule from a clock. The DNR example rules out the most naive selector.
A finer classification could separate the difficulty of finding a clock,
finding a closed subclass, and selecting a path inside that subclass.
This is especially relevant to proof-producing synthesis systems, where
``there exists a strategy'' and ``a strategy can be extracted uniformly''
are operationally very different outputs.
\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 03, ``Structural classes with complete computable deadlines'':} Identify useful classes in which every recurrent path existence instance has
a computably bounded recurrent path. Finite-state regions satisfy a much
stronger lasso property, while \Cref{lbh:qd:thm:recnotcb} excludes a general
result. Intermediate possibilities include well-structured resource
restrictions, limited zero tests, and recurrence supported by computably
bounded return regions. Any proposed class needs a proved closure property
that excludes the halting-time tree mechanism.

\begin{question}[A numerically explicit universal quadratic relation; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-universal}
Instantiate the event-reflecting interpreter as a complete edge table,
compile its sparse polynomial, and publish the exact dimension, monomial
count, circuit size, and coefficient distribution. The first target should
be a reproducible and formally checked instance, not a premature claim of
minimality. A successful artifact would turn the current constructive
existence theorem into a directly executable fixed-polynomial benchmark.
\end{question}
\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``A completely expanded fixed universal law'':} \textbf{Target.} Supply a particular finite universal checkpoint machine,
run the compiler, publish its exact $D$, edge count, monomial count, and input
encoding, and prove a simulation theorem for that exact table. The current
report gives a fixed-law existence theorem and a generic finite-table compiler,
but not this final universal artifact. Optimizing $D$ while keeping height six
and binary branching is a meaningful quantitative problem.

\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~A's question ``Quantitative universal compilation'' and Report~B's
question \texttt{pqc:q:direct}.}
\begin{question}[Height reduction versus dimension; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-height}
The bound eight is sharp for this compiler because two parallel identical
increments contribute four overlapping quadratic residual terms. Can a
modified encoding attain coefficient height two, or even one, while retaining
one equality, bounded branching, degree two, and a manageable fixed state
dimension? Variable splitting can redistribute coefficients, but must not
create unconstrained target coordinates or additional successors. The
appropriate result is a proved quantitative tradeoff, not merely a smaller
unexpanded expression.
\end{question}
\srcnote{\wtag{} Partly answered, for a different encoding, by source~11: its incoming-edge law
(\cref{lbh:ql:thm:compiler}) has height at most~$6$ and drops the $q$ control coordinates. It does
not reach height two or one, and it changes the encoding, so the question for source~03's
encoding stays open (\cref{lbh:sec:threecompilers}).}
\begin{question}[When are edge tags dispensable?; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-tags}
Tags guarantee that labelled choices remain distinct. For edge tables whose
enabled labelled edges always have different control-and-counter targets,
a tag-free encoding may be possible. Characterize the exact semantic
condition and the minimal extra coordinates needed when only some edges
collide. Preserve recurrence and the computable-deadline distinctions, not
only reachability.
\end{question}
\srcnote{\wtag{} Source~11 answers the dual question: given incoming-edge tags, the control
coordinates are dispensable (\cref{lbh:ql:thm:compiler}). Whether tags can be dropped stays open.}
\begin{question}[Binary fairness compilation; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-fairness}
The explicit fairness construction raises the outdegree from two to three.
Can the same one-always-enabled-action specification be retained in a
binary graph without introducing fair infinite runs that only traverse
selection gadgets? A correct binary construction must account for
continuous enabling during every intermediate state and must preserve the
lock-based progress argument.
\end{question}
\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``Fairness as a compiler parameter'':} \textbf{Question.} Extend the incoming-edge method to multiple progress
obligations and distinguish weak fairness, strong fairness, and generalized
B\"uchi acceptance. A round-robin finite-control monitor handles finitely many
B\"uchi sets qualitatively, but deadline bounds must be transformed explicitly.
Scheduler fairness should not be conflated with the existence of an arbitrary
recurrent branch; Harel's classical fairness results are the appropriate
starting point \cite{harel1986}.

\begin{question}[One-dimensional and low-dimensional boundaries; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-lowdim}
The affine obstruction is dimension sensitive: for $D\geq2$ it forces a
finite target set, while $D=1$ is functional. Determine stronger quantitative
restrictions for quadratic graphs in very small dimension, under the same
uniform branching and marker assumptions. This is different from reducing
the number of registers in a counter model, because the present encoding
also spends coordinates on controls and edge tags.
\end{question}
\begin{question}[Integer rather than natural coordinates; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-integer}
The compiler exploits nonnegative products. A replacement over unrestricted
integers needs an explicit nonnegativity encoding or a different local
construction. Standard changes of domain can alter degree, dimension, and
branching multiplicities simultaneously. A useful target is a carefully
stated integer-coordinate analogue that preserves recurrence and has a
proved global successor bound, rather than a domain substitution that
silently introduces many representations of one state.
\end{question}
\par\smallskip\noindent\textit{Overlapping question of source 11, ``Optimal degree under stronger geometric restrictions'':} \textbf{Question.} What is the minimum degree and dimension when the local
polynomial must be globally nonnegative on all real inputs, not merely on the
nonnegative orthant, and must remain witness-free and binary-branching? The
quartic construction is an upper bound. The degree-one obstruction does not
settle the globally nonnegative quadratic case, whose zero set has a much
more rigid affine geometry. Any claim of degree optimality there needs a
separate theorem.

\srcnote{\wtag{} The two formulations ask the same beyond-the-orthant question from two sides:
integer coordinates (source~03) and global nonnegativity on all real inputs (source~11). Compare
Report~A's question \texttt{cdc:q:degree}, whose integer-domain part is related.}
\begin{question}[Formalizing the infinite semantic boundary; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-formal}
Prove the compiler in Lean first, then the deadline compactness equivalence
and both explicit separating trees. These examples make useful regression
tests for the intended theorem statements: an incorrect compactness lemma
would derive recurrence for~\eqref{lbh:qd:eq:small}, while an incorrect effective
choice lemma would produce a computable branch through $T_D$. A full
formalization should record separately finite trace arithmetization,
classical infinite existence, and computability of witnesses.
\end{question}
\srcnote{\wtag{} Compare Report~A's question \texttt{cdc:q:verified}, and the formalization plans
of \cref{lbh:sec:formalization}, \cref{lbh:qd:sec:proveit} and \cref{lbh:ql:sec:repository}.}
\begin{question}[Optimal incomplete certificate families; source 03]\label[question]{lbh:q:qd-incomplete}
A complete finite certificate family is impossible for the universal sets,
but useful incomplete families can still be compared. Study which
recurrent programs are certified by periodicity, monotone recurrence,
finite-state abstractions, or computable return maps, and prove strict
inclusions between these classes. The relevant optimization criterion is
coverage of meaningful subclasses together with a small trusted checker,
not an unattainable complete positive checker for every recurrent input.
\end{question}
\begin{question}[Sparse and low-interaction versions; source 11]\label[question]{lbh:q:ql-sparse}
\textbf{Question.} Can the same exact path semantics be realized by a quadratic
polynomial in which every coordinate interacts with only a bounded number of
other coordinates, with a controlled state-dimension increase? The global
one-hot sums create dense selector interactions. Replacing them with local
flow gadgets must preserve complete successor lists and must not introduce
unbounded auxiliary choices. The relevant invariant is path correspondence,
not just existential satisfiability.
\end{question}
\section{Conclusion}

The decisive boundary in Diophantine compilation is not the aesthetic form
of the computational substrate. It is the information content and
quantifier structure of the requested behavior. Finite successful
computations are c.e. and hence Diophantine. Recurrent behavior requires
coherent infinite choices, and finite branching alone does not remove that
requirement.

A shared computable progress deadline supplies precisely the compactness
needed to pass from coherent finite certificates to an infinite execution.
It is equivalent to a nonempty effectively closed family of accepting
schedules and, through the classical basis theorem, to the existence of an
HIF accepting schedule. The degree-spectrum construction gives a separate
quantitative answer: every c.e. degree can be exactly the minimum oracle
strength needed to compute a successful clock. The halting-degree instance
has accepting runs but no computable deadline at all.

These results coexist with a completely explicit finite arithmetization.
The two-stack compiler uses natural witnesses and quadratic residuals,
producing a nonnegative quartic with a proved labelled-trace bijection.
The same computations occur in a compact rational guarded affine system;
adding a real scheduler coordinate turns it into a deterministic map while
making the initial-parameter quantifier visible. Fixed-arity MRDP then
provides the correct alternating polynomial signatures, rather than an
invalid universal existential representation of liveness.

The immediate research value is a precise architecture for extending
ProveIt: exact finite compilers, coherent deadline trees, separately audited
basis and degree arguments, and explicit arithmetic quantifier boundaries.
This architecture makes both positive representations and impossibility
results testable against the intended operational semantics.
\section{Conclusion\srctag{03}}

A single quadratic equation over natural source and target coordinates can
express a universal local computational substrate without existential
transition witnesses and without losing a uniform binary branching bound.
The degree is optimal under these precise constraints. Nevertheless,
small-degree local arithmetic does not collapse infinite behavior to finite
arithmetic evidence.

The exact distinction is
\[
 \text{computable recurrent run}
 \ \Longrightarrow\
 \text{computably bounded recurrent run}
 \ \Longrightarrow\
 \text{recurrent run},
\]
with both implications strict. Prescribed computable deadlines restore a
closed finite-prefix condition and yield a $\Pi^0_1$ description; choosing
some computable deadline raises the complexity to $\Sigma^0_3$; removing the
effectivity restriction on the infinite witness reaches $\Sigma^1_1$.
These distinctions survive faithful effective implementations and remain
visible in an explicit low-degree polynomial normal form.

For ProveIt, the practical lesson is architectural. Reuse the existing
finite-trace Diophantine theorems, add an exact local compiler, and keep the
infinite-path quantifiers explicit. The boundary itself is a useful theorem:
it identifies where more clever finite arithmetization can help, and where
uniform finite positive certification is mathematically impossible.
\section{Conclusion\srctag{11}}
A single local quadratic law can have an elementary one-step relation,
a computable finite successor list, and at most two successors, while the
existence of an infinitely recurrent run is analytic-complete. Low polynomial
degree is therefore not a measure of the logical complexity of all temporal
observations of the resulting system.

The decisive additional structure is not merely ``more finite witnesses.''
It is compatibility. Prescribed computable deadlines turn recurrence into a
closed finite-prefix condition and admit exact quadratic certificates at each
horizon. Existentially supplying an algorithm for the deadline has the sharp
$\Sigma^0_3$ cost proved here. Supplying a computable run has that same index-set
complexity but is a strictly stronger demand on an individual instance.

These distinctions are preserved by exact rational planar stack dynamics.
They give the Diophantine research program a concrete next step beyond another
existential representation of finite halting: build compilers whose contracts
include the kind of infinite witness they preserve, and make the quantifiers
visible in the theorem statement.

\section{Source 12 and its place in this report}\label{lbh:cs:sec:source}

\subsection{The manuscript}\label{lbh:cs:sec:ms12}
\wtag{} \emph{Beyond Enumerable Clock Cones: Limit Computations, Unique-Path Trees, and Quadratic
Diophantine Progress. Two-stack machines and exact rational affine substrates.} Research article
and executable certificate package; author line as delivered: ``Prepared for Vladimir Reshetnikov /
OpenAI ChatGPT'', dated October 2, 2026 (a 23-page PDF, not shipped). It is batch~79, manuscript~04
of ProveIt's incoming reports, delivered as \path{clock_spectra_research.zip} in arrival commit
\texttt{060e08a07} and placed in commit \texttt{bbaf322e5}. It is called \emph{source~12} here, after
the prefix \path{12-clock-spectra-} of its shipped files; the number continues this report's own
file sequence and is not a batch number. Its pin is \texttt{e58b724c2}: it inspected this report at
that commit, where \path{article.tex} was the batch-62/63 text of Parts~I--V. The only later change
to that text before this write is the batch-78 note after the question ``Other unconventional
substrates with timing certificates'' in \cref{lbh:sec:questions}, which source~12 does not use.
Source~12 takes \cref{lbh:q:spectra} as its explicit research target.

Its title-page summary box read as follows.
\begin{quote}\small
\textbf{A concrete repository question.} Which degrees can be the least
computational resource sufficient to supply deadlines for an infinite
recurrent computation? The inspected report establishes computably
enumerable cones and asks for extensions.

\textbf{Answers developed here.} Every limit-computable degree is realizable.
Every degree of a unique path through a recursive Baire-space tree is
realizable, including the jump hierarchies explicitly constructed below.
Finite unions of incomparable cones yield spectra with no least degree.
Every least degree, if one exists, contains a self-modulus and is
hyperarithmetic.

\textbf{Arithmetic realization.} A natural-number quadratic polynomial
has zeros in bijection with the legal labelled two-stack runs satisfying
all prescribed finite deadlines. Its helpers, including those belonging
to inactive rules, are unique. An exact rational affine realization
preserves the same clock spectra.
\end{quote}
Its status statement:
\begin{quote}\small
\textbf{Status and attribution.} This is an unrefereed AI-generated research
manuscript. All main claims have mathematical proofs below; finite exact
Python checks support the compiler implementation, not the infinitary
proofs. There is no Lean or Rocq verification in this package. Classical
unique-path, modulus, jump-hierarchy, and incomparability ingredients are
credited. The contribution is a constructive answer to specified parts of
the repository's question and its certificate realization; literature-wide
priority and a complete classification of least clock degrees are not claimed.
\end{quote}
Its abstract:
\begin{quote}\small
A progress clock for a nondeterministic machine is a total function $d$
such that one infinite run produces its $k$th accepting event by time
$d(k)$ for every $k$. The oracle supplying $d$ need not compute that run.
We study the degrees that can supply such clocks, rather than reclassifying
unrestricted recurrence.

We give a uniform realization of the bounding-degree spectrum of any
recursive tree on $\N$. Up to uniform computable transformations,
clock problems are precisely upward effectively closed mass problems
in Baire space. A recursive tree with unique path $f$ therefore yields
exactly the cone above $\csdegT(f)$. For any computable approximation to a
$\Delta^0_2$ set $A$, a first-matching-stage construction produces such a
tree with unique path Turing equivalent to $A$; convergence moduli are not
assumed computable from $A$. A second, deferred-oracle-check construction
produces unique paths coding effective transfinite jump hierarchies.
Finite and effective countable disjoint sums give nonprincipal spectra.
Conversely, any least clock degree contains a self-modulus, and the
classical modulus theorem implies that it is hyperarithmetic.

To realize the results arithmetically, we give a guarded-affine quadratic
gadget with a unique natural lift and an explicit two-stack compiler.
For $m$ rules, external horizon $T$, and $k$ deadlines, its quadratic has
$3(T+1)+9mT+k$ natural coordinates and a zero-set bijection with labelled
runs. Its exact rational affine counterpart acts on a coded subset of
$[0,1]^3$ with identical event times. The paper distinguishes this growing
family of finite quadratics from a fixed-arity Diophantine representation,
and includes executable code, exact test results, and further research
questions.
\end{quote}
\wtag{} The delivered author line is quoted above as delivered, as the batch-79 records ask; for
sources~10, 03 and~11 the batch-62 write replaced the author lines by a neutral description
(\cref{lbh:sec:ms10}). The delivered README and source audit (the latter shipped verbatim as
\path{12-clock-spectra-source_audit.md}) add no theorem.

\paragraph{An extension of \cref{lbh:thm:ce-spectrum}.} Every c.e.\ set is $\Delta^0_2$, so the
spectrum equation \eqref{lbh:eq:spectrum} for c.e.\ degrees is the c.e.\ case of source~12's
\cref{lbh:cs:cor:delta}, each for its own fixed two-stack system (completeness or realization for
one fixed system is not automatically a statement about another, \cref{lbh:conv:union}). The
routes differ: source~10's tree $T_A$ has every majorant of the enumeration modulus as a path, while
source~12's tree $T_a$ has exactly one path. The decisive inequality is the same:
\eqref{lbh:cs:eq:clock-bound} is the step $g(i)\le\tau_f(i+1)\le d(i+1)$ in the proof of
\cref{lbh:thm:ce-spectrum}. Source~12 does not restate source~10's least-\emph{scheduler}-degree
clause. For its singleton trees it follows by the argument of that proof: a schedule of the
explorer computes the unique path from its completed unary guesses, and the path computes a schedule
that makes exactly those guesses. This sentence is an observation of the write, not a statement of
source~12. \Cref{lbh:cor:halting-spectrum} is the case $A=\emptyset'$.

\paragraph{A degree-two form of \cref{lbh:thm:compiler}.} Source~12's \cref{lbh:cs:thm:quadratic}
proves the conclusion of source~10's two-stack compiler at total degree two instead of four: the
natural zeros are in bijection with the rule-labelled legal runs of the horizon that meet every
prescribed deadline, and every auxiliary coordinate, including the helpers of inactive rules, is
uniquely determined by the run. It is stronger in degree only, and the two theorems differ in
format:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabularx}{\textwidth}{@{}p{0.2\textwidth}XX@{}}
\toprule
 & Source 10, \cref{lbh:thm:compiler} & Source 12, \cref{lbh:cs:thm:quadratic}\\
\midrule
rules & stack symbols $1,3$; guards unrestricted, empty, top-$1$, top-$3$; updates stay, push,
pop (a pop needs a top guard) & binary stacks; per stack one of stay, empty test, push $b$,
pop $b$ (the pop tests the top bit)\\
stack code & $\nu$, base four, $\nu(\epsilon)=1$ \eqref{lbh:eq:stackcode} & binary with a leading
sentinel, minus one; empty stack $0$\\
degree & $4$: a sum of squares of quadratic residuals & $2$: squares of affine forms plus the
unsquared products $ab$ and $s(a+b)$ of \cref{lbh:cs:lem:gadget}\\
where it is nonnegative & every real point & every point with $s,a,b\ge0$; the bijection is proved
over $\N$ only: the gadget lemma fails for a signed helper, and the bijection fails over
$\Q_{\ge0}$ (the review's examples after the domain paragraph of \cref{lbh:cs:sec:quadratic})\\
initial configuration & substituted as constants & three coordinates retained (three fewer if
substituted, as source~12 notes)\\
deadlines & $1\le d_1<\cdots<d_k\le H$ & $0\le d_i\le T$, in any order\\
coordinates & $H(3+R+G)+k$ & $3(T+1)+9mT+k$ ($m$ rules)\\
residuals & $H(4R+E+2G+2)+k$ listed quadratic residuals & one polynomial with $3+T+4mT+k$ summands
(counted at this write)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabularx}
\end{center}
For the same machine ($m=R$, at most $2R$ top guards) source~12 uses more coordinates: it pays
$8mT$ sign helpers to lower the degree, as it says itself. Its cubic variant
(\cref{lbh:cs:sec:quadratic}, last paragraph) has $3(T+1)+mT+k$ coordinates. Degree two for
\emph{counter} machines was already reached in Part~III without auxiliaries (source~03's
\cref{lbh:qd:thm:compiler}, source~11's \cref{lbh:ql:thm:compiler}) and, with deadlines, by
\cref{lbh:ql:cor:deadlinepoly}; the comparison of \cref{lbh:sec:threecompilers} said that source~10's
two-stack trace needs degree four, and source~12 removes that difference for two-stack traces. The
gadget $G(D,s,a,b)=(D-a+b)^2+ab+s(a+b)$ is a compact form, in one natural-domain term, of the
canonical positive and negative parts gated by a selector in Report~A's canonical branch activation
(\texttt{cdc:wf:lem:activation} in \path{canonical-diophantine-certificates}). None of this improves
the operation count of a fixed universal polynomial (\cref{lbh:cs:sec:quasi}).

\paragraph{What source 12 answers.} \Cref{lbh:q:spectra} asks which upward-closed classes of
degrees occur as clock spectra of single inputs of a fixed two-stack system, and which degrees can
be least. Source~12 answers it in part. Every $\Delta^0_2$ degree, every degree of a unique path
through a recursive Baire-space tree, and in particular $\mathbf0^{(r)}$ and $\mathbf0^{(\omega)}$,
is a least clock degree (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:delta,lbh:cs:cor:singleton,lbh:cs:thm:jump-tree});
spectra with exactly two minimal degrees and no least degree exist (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:nonprincipal});
a least degree must contain a self-modulus and is hyperarithmetic (\cref{lbh:cs:thm:least}), and no
nonzero hyperimmune-free degree is least (\cref{lbh:cs:cor:hif}). Up to uniform computable
transformations the clock problems are exactly the upward effectively closed mass problems
(\cref{lbh:cs:thm:closed}). It leaves open which inclusions of \eqref{lbh:cs:eq:gap} are equalities,
and its remark after \cref{lbh:cs:thm:closed} says that it does not claim that every upward
collection of degrees has an effectively closed presentation. The commentary after
\cref{lbh:q:spectra} warns that an arbitrary limit approximation need not have a convergence modulus
computable from its limit; source~12 does not contradict this but avoids it, since first matching
stages are not settling times (\cref{lbh:cs:thm:firstmatch}, \cref{lbh:cs:ex:early}). A dated note
under \cref{lbh:q:spectra} records the answer there.
